Hi, yeah, thanks for having me. Um, I guess what finally made me ask for help is that I just keep forgetting these little things that I normally handle without an issue. Like, I'm a secretary, so I'm usually pretty used to juggling stuff, but lately I keep forgetting to return phone calls, or I'll completely forget to turn in a registration form. Or even just stopping for an errand on the way home... which, actually, speaking of the drive home, they just started all that construction on the interstate and it has been an absolute nightmare. I sat behind this massive cement mixer for like half an hour yesterday, and my boss was complaining about the exact same thing this morning. He always uses that hazelnut creamer for his coffee and he was just going on and on about the traffic while he poured it, spilling it a little on the counter, which I usually have to wipe up. But, um... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, yeah. Just forgetting to stop for errands and stuff like that. It's just getting really frustrating.

Um, confused about where I am? No, I don't think so. I mean, I always know what day it is, mostly because I have to write the date on all the incoming mail at work. Um, well, I guess I'm still getting by, mostly. But it's... it's definitely causing some friction. Like, at work, I've missed a few small follow-ups recently. Just little things, like forgetting to email a client back to confirm a meeting time, or misplacing the sticky note where I wrote down a phone message. Speaking of sticky notes, I bought these really cute ones shaped like little cats the other day. They were on clearance at the craft store. I went in there just looking for some new yarn because I've been trying to learn how to crochet. My grandmother used to crochet these huge, beautiful afghans, and I always wanted to learn. I watched a bunch of YouTube tutorials, but my tension is always too tight, so my squares end up looking like little triangles. It's so frustrating. But anyway, the cat sticky notes are adorable, even if they don't stick very well. 

But, um, yeah, at work... I worry about it. My boss hasn't said anything major, but I'm hoping to move up to an Office Manager role eventually, and I just know that missing these little details could affect my reputation. And it happens at home, too, with my kids' school deadlines. I forgot to sign a permission slip last week until the morning of the field trip, and it was this whole mad dash to find the paper while making lunches. My son wanted turkey and cheese, but we were out of turkey, so I had to make him ham, which he hates, and he was so cranky about it... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the memory stuff. Yeah, it's just... I'm getting by, but I worry it's going to hold me back.

Um, remembering how to do normal things? No, I don't think so. I mean, my routines are pretty ingrained. Like making coffee in the morning, I could do that in my sleep. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, feeling little pleasure? No, not really. I mean, I still like doing all my normal stuff. Like I said, I've been trying to learn to crochet, and even though the squares are coming out weird, I still like sitting down with the yarn. I actually went back to that craft store yesterday to see if they had any different hooks. I thought maybe a bigger hook would help with my tension problem. The lady working there was so nice, she showed me this whole aisle of ergonomic hooks. They have these squishy handles that are supposed to be better for your hands. My mom used to get such bad arthritis in her hands from knitting, she had to stop making sweaters. She knitted me this gorgeous cable-knit sweater when I was in high school, it was this deep burgundy color. I wore it all the time until I accidentally shrank it in the wash. I was so upset. I tried stretching it back out while it was wet, like you're supposed to, but it never really fit right again. I ended up giving it to my niece. She's only ten, so it fits her perfectly. She wore it to school last week and spilled juice on it, though. Kids, right? You can't give them anything nice. But, um... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, enjoying things. Yeah, no, I haven't had any days like that. I'm still enjoying my hobbies and everything just fine.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, feeling down or low? No, I don't think so. I mean, I get stressed out, obviously, like with the kids and work and trying to remember everything, but I wouldn't say I feel down. I'm generally pretty upbeat. Actually, my friend Sarah was just telling me the other day that I always seem so cheerful, even when things are chaotic. We were at the park with our kids, and her youngest was having this massive meltdown because he wanted to go down the slide backwards and she wouldn't let him. It was a whole scene. He's three, and three-year-olds are just... they're a lot. My kids are older now, eight and ten, so we're past the toddler tantrums, thank goodness. But now it's all about homework and friend drama. My daughter came home crying yesterday because her best friend told her she couldn't sit with her at lunch. Girls can be so mean at that age. I remember when I was in fifth grade, this girl named Jessica started a rumor that I picked my nose, and nobody would talk to me for a week. It was awful. I ended up hiding in the library during recess. The librarian, Mrs. Higgins, she was so sweet. She let me help her stamp the due date cards in the back of the books. Do they even use those cards anymore? Everything is digital now. I kind of miss the smell of old library books, though. There's just something about it. But um... yeah, no, I haven't been feeling low or anything like that. Just the usual stress, but no real down days.

We were at the park with our kids, and her youngest was having this massive meltdown because he wanted to go down the slide backwards and she wouldn't let him. It was a whole scene. He's three, and three-year-olds are just... they're a lot. My kids are older now, eight and ten, so we're past the toddler tantrums, thank goodness. But now it's all about homework and friend drama. My daughter came home crying yesterday because her best friend told her she couldn't sit with her at lunch. Girls can be so mean at that age. I remember when I was in fifth grade, this girl named Jessica started a rumor that I picked my nose, and nobody would talk to me for a week. It was awful. I ended up hiding in the library during recess. The librarian, Mrs. Higgins, she was so sweet. She let me help her stamp the due date cards in the back of the books. Do they even use those cards anymore? Everything is digital now. I kind of miss the smell of old library books, though. There's just something about it. But um... yeah, no, I haven't been feeling low or anything like that. Just the usual stress, but no real down days.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had any moments recently where you couldn't remember how to do something you've done many times before, like following a recipe, using a familiar appliance, or finding your way around your own neighborhood?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 36 / secretary_or_administrative_assistant / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, forgetting how to do things? No, I don't think I've had that happen. Like I said before with the coffee maker, I'm pretty good with my routines. I did try a new recipe the other night, though. It was this chicken piccata thing I saw on Pinterest. Have you ever made that? It has capers in it. I'd never actually bought capers before, they come in this tiny little jar that's incredibly hard to open. I had to run it under hot water for like five minutes just to get the lid loose. My husband usually opens jars for me, but he was working late. He's an accountant, so this time of year is just crazy for him with tax season coming up. He basically lives at the office from February to April. I always tell him he needs to take a break and at least eat a decent dinner, which is why I was making the chicken piccata. But then I realized halfway through that I didn't have any lemons, and you kind of need lemon juice for it, right? So I had to substitute lime juice, which actually wasn't terrible, but it definitely tasted a little like a margarita. We ended up ordering pizza anyway because the kids wouldn't even touch it. They only eat chicken if it's in nugget form. It's so frustrating. But, um... what was the question? Oh, forgetting how to do things. No, no, I haven't forgotten how to use anything or get around. I know my neighborhood like the back of my hand.

Um, remembering recent conversations? No, not really. If I'm actually paying attention, I remember things just fine. It's just when I'm trying to do three things at once that I forget what someone told me. But if we're just talking, I remember. My boss was so mad because we just got it for the breakroom last month. I was sitting there yesterday for probably fifteen minutes just listening to the radio. They were playing all these old 90s songs, which really took me back. I haven't heard some of those since high school. We used to drive around with the windows down blasting that music... but, oh, what were you asking? Energy? Yeah, no, my energy is fine. I'm not any more tired than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself confused about where you are, or had trouble knowing the date or time, even when you're not distracted or busy?\n\nPatient: Um, confused about where I am? No, I don't think so. I mean, I always know what day it is, mostly because I have to write the date on all the incoming mail at work. We get so much mail, you wouldn't believe it. Catalogs, mostly, which is so weird because who buys office supplies from a catalog anymore? It's all online. But my boss still likes to flip through them. He's a little old-school like that. He still uses a Rolodex, if you can believe it! I tried to show him how to save contacts on his phone, but he said the screen is too small and his fingers are too big. He's a nice guy, though. He let me leave early last Friday because my car was making this weird rattling noise. It sounded like a bunch of rocks in a tin can every time I hit the brakes. Turns out it was just a loose heat shield, which the mechanic fixed for like twenty bucks. I was so relieved, I thought I was going to need new brakes or something. But, um... no, I don't get confused about where I am or the time or anything. Just the normal forgetting where I put my keys type of stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to enjoy don't seem to give you as much pleasure or satisfaction anymore?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. Like I was saying before, I still enjoy things just fine. I still love watching my shows and, you know, going out. My husband and I actually went to this new Italian place over on 5th Street last weekend. It used to be a shoe store, I think? Or maybe a dry cleaner. Anyway, they completely remodeled it, and it looks so nice inside. They have these really cool Edison bulbs hanging from the ceiling, which I love, but they always make me wonder how much electricity they use. My electric bill was so high last month, I almost called the company to complain. I mean, we didn't even run the AC that much because it's been pretty mild. But my husband always leaves the TV on when he falls asleep on the couch, so maybe that's it. I keep telling him to set the sleep timer, but he never remembers. He's worse than I am sometimes! But, um... yeah, no, I still enjoy everything I used to. I haven't noticed any change there.\n\nDoctor: Has your memory trouble interfered with your ability to do your job well or caused any problems in your relationships or other daily activities?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess I'm still getting by, mostly. But it's... it's definitely causing some friction. Like, at work, I've missed a few small follow-ups recently. Just little things, like forgetting to email a client back to confirm a meeting time, or misplacing the sticky note where I wrote down a phone message. Speaking of sticky notes, I bought these really cute ones shaped like little cats the other day. They were on clearance at the craft store. I went in there just looking for some new yarn because I've been trying to learn how to crochet. My grandmother used to crochet these huge, beautiful afghans, and I always wanted to learn. I watched a bunch of YouTube tutorials, but my tension is always too tight, so my squares end up looking like little triangles. It's so frustrating. But anyway, the cat sticky notes are adorable, even if they don't stick very well. \n\nBut, um, yeah, at work... I worry about it. My boss hasn't said anything major, but I'm hoping to move up to an Office Manager role eventually, and I just know that missing these little details could affect my reputation. And it happens at home, too, with my kids' school deadlines. I forgot to sign a permission slip last week until the morning of the field trip, and it was this whole mad dash to find the paper while making lunches. My son wanted turkey and cheese, but we were out of turkey, so I had to make him ham, which he hates, and he was so cranky about it... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the memory stuff. Yeah, it's just... I'm getting by, but I worry it's going to hold me back.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble remembering how to do things you normally do without thinking, like following a routine at work, using common tools, or navigating familiar places?\n\nPatient: Um, remembering how to do normal things? No, I don't think so. I mean, my routines are pretty ingrained. Like making coffee in the morning, I could do that in my sleep. Which is good because I am usually half asleep when I do it. I use one of those French presses now, though. I used to have a regular drip machine, but it broke right before Thanksgiving last year. Water just started leaking all over the counter. It was a disaster, especially because my in-laws were coming over and my father-in-law drinks coffee like it's water. We ended up having to run out to the coffee shop down the street, the one with the green awning? They make a really good mocha, but it's so expensive. I can't justify spending six dollars on a coffee every day. Anyway, I bought the French press because it was cheaper and doesn't plug in, so less to break, right? The only annoying part is cleaning the grounds out of the bottom. They get stuck in the little mesh filter thing. I read online you're not supposed to put coffee grounds down the sink, so I try to scrape them into the trash, but it's messy. But um... no, I haven't forgotten how to use it or anything like that. I'm fine with my usual routines and getting around and all that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt little pleasure in doing things you usually enjoy, even if just for a short while?\n\nPatient: Um, feeling little pleasure? No, not really. I mean, I still like doing all my normal stuff. Like I said, I've been trying to learn to crochet, and even though the squares are coming out weird, I still like sitting down with the yarn. I actually went back to that craft store yesterday to see if they had any different hooks. I thought maybe a bigger hook would help with my tension problem. The lady working there was so nice, she showed me this whole aisle of ergonomic hooks. They have these squishy handles that are supposed to be better for your hands. My mom used to get such bad arthritis in her hands from knitting, she had to stop making sweaters. She knitted me this gorgeous cable-knit sweater when I was in high school, it was this deep burgundy color. I wore it all the time until I accidentally shrank it in the wash. I was so upset. I tried stretching it back out while it was wet, like you're supposed to, but it never really fit right again. I ended up giving it to my niece. She's only ten, so it fits her perfectly. She wore it to school last week and spilled juice on it, though. Kids, right? You can't give them anything nice. But, um... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, enjoying things. Yeah, no, I haven't had any days like that. I'm still enjoying my hobbies and everything just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt down or low, even if only for a few hours?\n\nPatient: Um, feeling down or low? No, I don't think so. I mean, I get stressed out, obviously, like with the kids and work and trying to remember everything, but I wouldn't say I feel down. I'm generally pretty upbeat. Actually, my friend Sarah was just telling me the other day that I always seem so cheerful, even when things are chaotic. We were at the park with our kids, and her youngest was having this massive meltdown because he wanted to go down the slide backwards and she wouldn't let him. It was a whole scene. He's three, and three-year-olds are just... they're a lot. My kids are older now, eight and ten, so we're past the toddler tantrums, thank goodness. But now it's all about homework and friend drama. My daughter came home crying yesterday because her best friend told her she couldn't sit with her at lunch. Girls can be so mean at that age. I remember when I was in fifth grade, this girl named Jessica started a rumor that I picked my nose, and nobody would talk to me for a week. It was awful. I ended up hiding in the library during recess. The librarian, Mrs. Higgins, she was so sweet. She let me help her stamp the due date cards in the back of the books. Do they even use those cards anymore? Everything is digital now. I kind of miss the smell of old library books, though. There's just something about it. But um... yeah, no, I haven't been feeling low or anything like that. Just the usual stress, but no real down days.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any moments recently where you couldn't remember how to do something you've done many times before, like following a recipe, using a familiar appliance, or finding your way around your own neighborhood?\n\nPatient: Um, forgetting how to do things? No, I don't think I've had that happen. Like I said before with the coffee maker, I'm pretty good with my routines. I did try a new recipe the other night, though. It was this chicken piccata thing I saw on Pinterest. Have you ever made that? It has capers in it. I'd never actually bought capers before, they come in this tiny little jar that's incredibly hard to open. I had to run it under hot water for like five minutes just to get the lid loose. My husband usually opens jars for me, but he was working late. He's an accountant, so this time of year is just crazy for him with tax season coming up. He basically lives at the office from February to April. I always tell him he needs to take a break and at least eat a decent dinner, which is why I was making the chicken piccata. But then I realized halfway through that I didn't have any lemons, and you kind of need lemon juice for it, right? So I had to substitute lime juice, which actually wasn't terrible, but it definitely tasted a little like a margarita. We ended up ordering pizza anyway because the kids wouldn't even touch it. They only eat chicken if it's in nugget form. It's so frustrating. But, um... what was the question? Oh, forgetting how to do things. No, no, I haven't forgotten how to use anything or get around. I know my neighborhood like the back of my hand.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble remembering recent conversations or events, even when you weren't distracted at the time?\n\nPatient: Um, remembering recent conversations? No, not really. If I'm actually paying attention, I remember things just fine. It's just when I'm trying to do three things at once that I forget what someone told me. But if we're just talking, I remember. Like yesterday, my boss, Mr. Henderson, he called me into his office to talk about the new filing system. He's so particular about how everything is organized. He wants all the invoices color-coded by vendor, which honestly seems like overkill to me. I mean, we have a perfectly good alphabetical system right now. But he went to this seminar last weekend in Chicago, and apparently, the speaker was all about color-coding. Chicago is such a great city, though. My husband and I went there for our anniversary a few years ago. We stayed right near Navy Pier and went on this architecture boat tour. It was freezing, even though it was May, but the buildings were just beautiful. We had deep dish pizza, too, at Lou Malnati's. Everyone always argues about which place has the best deep dish, but that one was amazing. The crust was like a pastry. It took like 45 minutes to bake, though, so we were starving by the time it came out. We had to eat breadsticks just to survive the wait. But, um... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, remembering things. Yeah, if I'm not distracted, my memory is totally normal. I haven't noticed any weird memory problems or anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um, feeling like I couldn't get going or moving slowly? No, not really. I mean, I'm always on the go, honestly. If anything, I feel like I'm moving too fast sometimes, trying to keep up with everything. Speaking of moving fast, my daughter's soccer team had a tournament this weekend, and those girls are just non-stop. They played three games on Saturday alone. I was exhausted just watching them run back and forth across the field. It was freezing, too. The wind was whipping right across the bleachers, and I forgot my good winter coat. I only had this light fleece jacket I bought at Target on clearance a couple of years ago. It's super soft, but definitely not meant for sitting outside in November. I ended up having to buy a hot chocolate from the concession stand just to warm my hands. It was like four dollars for a tiny little cup, and it tasted like they just mixed powder with lukewarm water. Honestly, I make better hot chocolate at home. I use real milk and those little mini marshmallows. My son likes to put whipped cream on his, but I think that makes it too sweet. He has such a sweet tooth, just like my dad. My dad used to keep a stash of those butterscotch candies in his glove compartment, and he'd always sneak us one when my mom wasn't looking. She was very strict about sugar before dinner. But, um... what were we talking about? Oh, moving slowly. Yeah, no, I haven't felt slow or like I couldn't get going. Just the normal tired from running around so much.

No, I don't think so. I mean, I always know what day it is, mostly because I have to write the date on all the incoming mail at work. We get so much mail, you wouldn't believe it. Catalogs, mostly, which is so weird because who buys office supplies from a catalog anymore? It's all online. But my boss still likes to flip through them. He's a little old-school like that. He still uses a Rolodex, if you can believe it! I tried to show him how to save contacts on his phone, but he said the screen is too small and his fingers are too big. He's a nice guy, though. He let me leave early last Friday because my car was making this weird rattling noise. It sounded like a bunch of rocks in a tin can every time I hit the brakes. Turns out it was just a loose heat shield, which the mechanic fixed for like twenty bucks. I was so relieved, I thought I was going to need new brakes or something. But, um... no, I don't get confused about where I am or the time or anything. Just the normal forgetting where I put my keys type of stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to enjoy don't seem to give you as much pleasure or satisfaction anymore?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. Like I was saying before, I still enjoy things just fine. I still love watching my shows and, you know, going out. My husband and I actually went to this new Italian place over on 5th Street last weekend. It used to be a shoe store, I think? Or maybe a dry cleaner. Anyway, they completely remodeled it, and it looks so nice inside. They have these really cool Edison bulbs hanging from the ceiling, which I love, but they always make me wonder how much electricity they use. My electric bill was so high last month, I almost called the company to complain. I mean, we didn't even run the AC that much because it's been pretty mild. But my husband always leaves the TV on when he falls asleep on the couch, so maybe that's it. I keep telling him to set the sleep timer, but he never remembers. He's worse than I am sometimes! But, um... yeah, no, I still enjoy everything I used to. I haven't noticed any change there.\n\nDoctor: Has your memory trouble interfered with your ability to do your job well or caused any problems in your relationships or other daily activities?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess I'm still getting by, mostly. But it's... it's definitely causing some friction. Like, at work, I've missed a few small follow-ups recently. Just little things, like forgetting to email a client back to confirm a meeting time, or misplacing the sticky note where I wrote down a phone message. Speaking of sticky notes, I bought these really cute ones shaped like little cats the other day. They were on clearance at the craft store. I went in there just looking for some new yarn because I've been trying to learn how to crochet. My grandmother used to crochet these huge, beautiful afghans, and I always wanted to learn. I watched a bunch of YouTube tutorials, but my tension is always too tight, so my squares end up looking like little triangles. It's so frustrating. But anyway, the cat sticky notes are adorable, even if they don't stick very well. \n\nBut, um, yeah, at work... I worry about it. My boss hasn't said anything major, but I'm hoping to move up to an Office Manager role eventually, and I just know that missing these little details could affect my reputation. And it happens at home, too, with my kids' school deadlines. I forgot to sign a permission slip last week until the morning of the field trip, and it was this whole mad dash to find the paper while making lunches. My son wanted turkey and cheese, but we were out of turkey, so I had to make him ham, which he hates, and he was so cranky about it... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the memory stuff. Yeah, it's just... I'm getting by, but I worry it's going to hold me back.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble remembering how to do things you normally do without thinking, like following a routine at work, using common tools, or navigating familiar places?\n\nPatient: Um, remembering how to do normal things? No, I don't think so. I mean, my routines are pretty ingrained. Like making coffee in the morning, I could do that in my sleep. Which is good because I am usually half asleep when I do it. I use one of those French presses now, though. I used to have a regular drip machine, but it broke right before Thanksgiving last year. Water just started leaking all over the counter. It was a disaster, especially because my in-laws were coming over and my father-in-law drinks coffee like it's water. We ended up having to run out to the coffee shop down the street, the one with the green awning? They make a really good mocha, but it's so expensive. I can't justify spending six dollars on a coffee every day. Anyway, I bought the French press because it was cheaper and doesn't plug in, so less to break, right? The only annoying part is cleaning the grounds out of the bottom. They get stuck in the little mesh filter thing. I read online you're not supposed to put coffee grounds down the sink, so I try to scrape them into the trash, but it's messy. But um... no, I haven't forgotten how to use it or anything like that. I'm fine with my usual routines and getting around and all that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt little pleasure in doing things you usually enjoy, even if just for a short while?\n\nPatient: Um, feeling little pleasure? No, not really. I mean, I still like doing all my normal stuff. Like I said, I've been trying to learn to crochet, and even though the squares are coming out weird, I still like sitting down with the yarn. I actually went back to that craft store yesterday to see if they had any different hooks. I thought maybe a bigger hook would help with my tension problem. The lady working there was so nice, she showed me this whole aisle of ergonomic hooks. They have these squishy handles that are supposed to be better for your hands. My mom used to get such bad arthritis in her hands from knitting, she had to stop making sweaters. She knitted me this gorgeous cable-knit sweater when I was in high school, it was this deep burgundy color. I wore it all the time until I accidentally shrank it in the wash. I was so upset. I tried stretching it back out while it was wet, like you're supposed to, but it never really fit right again. I ended up giving it to my niece. She's only ten, so it fits her perfectly. She wore it to school last week and spilled juice on it, though. Kids, right? You can't give them anything nice. But, um... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, enjoying things. Yeah, no, I haven't had any days like that. I'm still enjoying my hobbies and everything just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt down or low, even if only for a few hours?\n\nPatient: Um, feeling down or low? No, I don't think so. I mean, I get stressed out, obviously, like with the kids and work and trying to remember everything, but I wouldn't say I feel down. I'm generally pretty upbeat. Actually, my friend Sarah was just telling me the other day that I always seem so cheerful, even when things are chaotic. We were at the park with our kids, and her youngest was having this massive meltdown because he wanted to go down the slide backwards and she wouldn't let him. It was a whole scene. He's three, and three-year-olds are just... they're a lot. My kids are older now, eight and ten, so we're past the toddler tantrums, thank goodness. But now it's all about homework and friend drama. My daughter came home crying yesterday because her best friend told her she couldn't sit with her at lunch. Girls can be so mean at that age. I remember when I was in fifth grade, this girl named Jessica started a rumor that I picked my nose, and nobody would talk to me for a week. It was awful. I ended up hiding in the library during recess. The librarian, Mrs. Higgins, she was so sweet. She let me help her stamp the due date cards in the back of the books. Do they even use those cards anymore? Everything is digital now. I kind of miss the smell of old library books, though. There's just something about it. But um... yeah, no, I haven't been feeling low or anything like that. Just the usual stress, but no real down days.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any moments recently where you couldn't remember how to do something you've done many times before, like following a recipe, using a familiar appliance, or finding your way around your own neighborhood?\n\nPatient: Um, forgetting how to do things? No, I don't think I've had that happen. Like I said before with the coffee maker, I'm pretty good with my routines. I did try a new recipe the other night, though. It was this chicken piccata thing I saw on Pinterest. Have you ever made that? It has capers in it. I'd never actually bought capers before, they come in this tiny little jar that's incredibly hard to open. I had to run it under hot water for like five minutes just to get the lid loose. My husband usually opens jars for me, but he was working late. He's an accountant, so this time of year is just crazy for him with tax season coming up. He basically lives at the office from February to April. I always tell him he needs to take a break and at least eat a decent dinner, which is why I was making the chicken piccata. They only eat chicken if it's in nugget form. It's so frustrating. But, um... what was the question? Oh, forgetting how to do things. No, no, I haven't forgotten how to use anything or get around. I know my neighborhood like the back of my hand.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble remembering recent conversations or events, even when you weren't distracted at the time?\n\nPatient: Um, remembering recent conversations? No, not really. If I'm actually paying attention, I remember things just fine. It's just when I'm trying to do three things at once that I forget what someone told me. But if we're just talking, I remember. Like yesterday, my boss, Mr. Henderson, he called me into his office to talk about the new filing system. He's so particular about how everything is organized. He wants all the invoices color-coded by vendor, which honestly seems like overkill to me. I mean, we have a perfectly good alphabetical system right now. But he went to this seminar last weekend in Chicago, and apparently, the speaker was all about color-coding. Chicago is such a great city, though. My husband and I went there for our anniversary a few years ago. We stayed right near Navy Pier and went on this architecture boat tour. It was freezing, even though it was May, but the buildings were just beautiful. We had deep dish pizza, too, at Lou Malnati's. Everyone always argues about which place has the best deep dish, but that one was amazing. The crust was like a pastry. Speaking of moving fast, my daughter's soccer team had a tournament this weekend, and those girls are just non-stop. They played three games on Saturday alone. I was exhausted just watching them run back and forth across the field. It was freezing, too. The wind was whipping right across the bleachers, and I forgot my good winter coat. I only had this light fleece jacket I bought at Target on clearance a couple of years ago. It's super soft, but definitely not meant for sitting outside in November.
